The input file uses `dll_nodata_def.h`, so the Coq lib is `dll_nodata_shape_lib`.

Let me analyze the code:

```c
struct list *multi_append(struct list *x, struct list *y, struct list *z)
/*@ Require dlistrep_shape(x,0) * dlistrep_shape(y,0) * dlistrep_shape(z,0)
    Ensure  dlistrep_shape(__return,0)
 */
{
    struct list *t, *u;
    if (x == 0) {
        t = append(y , z);
        return t;
    } else {
        t = x;
        u = t->next;
        /* INFILL */
        while (u) {
            if (y) {
              t -> next = y;
              y -> prev = t;
              t = y;
              y = y -> next;
              t -> next = u;
              u -> prev = t;
              if (y) {
                y -> prev = 0;
              }
              t = u;
              u = u -> next;
            }
            else {
              u = append(u , z);
              t -> next = u;
              return x;   
            }
        }
        u = append(y,z);
        t -> next = u;
      	if (u) {
          u -> prev = t;
        }
        return x;
    }
}
```

This is a multi_append function. The key path (else branch):
- t = x, u = t->next (so u = x->next)
- Loop: while (u) { ... }
  - If y != 0: insert y between t and u, advance both t and u
  - t -> next = y; y -> prev = t; t = y; y = y->next; t -> next = u; u -> prev = t; if (y) y->prev = 0; t = u; u = u->next;
  - If y == 0: u = append(u, z); t -> next = u; return x;
- After loop: u = append(y, z); t -> next = u; if (u) u->prev = t; return x;

So in the if branch, we're inserting nodes from y into the x list one at a time. When y runs out, we append z to u and return with an early return.

The traversal variables are t and u. They advance through the x list. When we insert a node from y, we temporarily set t = y, then y advances, then t = u and u = u->next.

Before insert:
- t -> next = u, u -> prev = t

1. t->next = y (t now points to y)
2. y->prev = t (y points back to t)

**Loop purpose**: The loop inserts nodes from the y list into the x list, one at a time, between t and u. t and u advance through the x list. When y becomes NULL, the else branch calls append(u, z) and returns x.

This is like a merge/interleave operation: each node from y is inserted between the current t and u in x.

   Actually, wait. I need to also think about the field expression vs exists variable issue. Looking at the invariant structure:

   `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u) * dlistrep_shape(u, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)`

   The boundary between dllseg and dlistrep:
   - dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u): segment from x to u (not including), t is last node in segment, t->next = u
   - dlistrep_shape(u, t): list starting at u with prev = t

   Now, in the loop body, we access:
   - t->next = y → t is arg[0] of... neither predicate directly. But t is arg[3] of dllseg_shape. Strategies would need to access t from within dllseg_shape.

   Hmm, this is getting complex. Let me think about what arguments expose the right fields for the loop body to work.

   In the loop body:
   1. `t -> next = y;` — writes t->next. t needs data_at permission.
   2. `y -> prev = t;` — writes y->prev. y needs data_at permission.
   3. `t = y;` — just assignment, needs t's stack cell (covered by t being in invariant)
   4. `y = y -> next;` — reads y->next. y needs data_at. y is in dlistrep_shape(y, 0), so y->next can be unfolded from dlistrep_shape.
   5. `t -> next = u;` — writes t->next. t needs data_at.
   6. `u -> prev = t;` — writes u->prev. u needs data_at.
   7. `y -> prev = 0;` — writes y->prev. y needs data_at.
   8. `t = u;` — assignment
   9. `u = u -> next;` — reads u->next. u is arg[0] of dlistrep_shape(u, t).

   So the key accesses are:
   - t->next (write) — need data_at for t->next
   - y->prev (write) — need data_at for y->prev
   - u->prev (write) — need data_at for u->prev
   - y->next (read) — need data_at for y->next
   - u->next (read) — need data_at for u->next

   For u: it's arg[0] of dlistrep_shape(u, t). The dlistrep_shape can be unfolded to expose u->next and u->prev. Since u->prev = t, unfolding dlistrep_shape(u, t) gives data_at(u->next, ...) and data_at(u->prev, t). 

   For y: it's arg[0] of dlistrep_shape(y, 0). Similarly, unfolding gives y->next and y->prev.

   For t: t is NOT the first argument of any shape predicate! t appears as arg[3] of dllseg_shape and arg[2] of dlistrep_shape. Neither makes t the first argument.

   This is the key issue. t is a critical pointer that gets dereferenced (`t->next = y`, `t->next = u`), but it doesn't appear as arg[0] in any shape predicate.

   How do we get data_at for t? Looking at SKILL.md section on fix strategy 1:
   "Put the dereferenced variable at arg[0] of a shape predicate"

   So I need to ensure t appears as arg[0] somewhere. Options:
   - Use field expressions: dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, u) could expose t->prev... but that's not what we want.
   - Split differently.

   Actually, looking at the dllseg_shape definition: 
   `dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y)` where x is the start, yp is the last node in the segment.
   
   dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u) has t as the last node (yp). The segment unfolds through x until it reaches t. At the last step before t: we'd need t->next = u and t->prev. The dllseg_shape unfolds recursively from the first argument (x) through all nodes until reaching yp=t.

   When strategies unfold dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u), they produce:
   - data_at(x->next, ..., z) * data_at(x->prev, ..., 0) * dllseg_shape(z, x, t, u)
   
   And continue until reaching t. At the node before t, the unfold produces:
   - data_at(t->next, ..., u) * data_at(t->prev, ..., prev_t)

   So dllseg_shape DOES produce data_at for t (through its unfolding). The question is whether strategies handle this correctly when t is the yp parameter.

   Let me think about this differently. In the SKILL.md fix strategy 1, it says to put the dereferenced variable at arg[0]. So maybe I need to restructure the invariant to have t be arg[0] of something.

   Alternative structure:
   `dllseg_shape(x, 0, p, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, p) * ...`

   But that would mean t is the end of the segment, and u is gone. We need u for the rest-of-x.

   Actually, wait. Re-reading the fix strategy more carefully:

   "Put the dereferenced variable at arg[0] of a shape predicate: If Memory Read fails on p->prev or t->next, the node being dereferenced must be the first argument of a dlistrep_shape or the first argument's field expression."

   So t needs to be arg[0] of dlistrep_shape(t, ...) or a field expression in arg[0] position.

   Could I use:
   `dllseg_shape(x, 0, p, t->next) * dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)`

   Wait, t->next = u. So: dllseg_shape(x, 0, p, u) * dlistrep_shape(u, t) * ...
   
   But t is still not arg[0]. The issue is t.

   Actually, let me think about using field expressions on both sides, per fix strategy 4:
   "Widen the boundary with field expressions on both sides"

   Looking at the fix strategy 4 example: for a backward traversal loop:
   `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)`

   In our case (forward traversal inserting y nodes):
   - t advances forward, u = t->next advances forward
   - We need access to t->next
   
   Maybe:
   `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next) * dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)`

   Here dllseg_shape ends at t, with endpoint t->next (= u). And dlistrep_shape starts at t->next (= u). The boundary is self-adapting because after t changes, t->next automatically recalculates.

   But t is still not arg[0] of any predicate. However, in dllseg_shape, when unfolding, the last node is yp = t. And in the recursive unfold, when we reach t:
   - dllseg_shape(t, prev_t, t, t->next): since current == yp (t == t), this would... let me re-check the definition.
   
   `dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y) = (x == y && xp == yp && emp) || ...`
   
   So dllseg_shape(t, prev_t, t, t->next): x = t, y = t->next. t != t->next (unless self-loop, which doesn't happen). So x != y. Then we unfold: data_at(t->next, ..., z) * data_at(t->prev, ..., prev_t) * dllseg_shape(z, t, t, t->next).
   
   But t is the yp (last node), and z = t->next. So dllseg_shape(t->next, t, t, t->next): now x = t->next, y = t->next. x == y. xp = t, yp = t. xp == yp? Yes. So this becomes emp. The unfold gives data_at for t->next = z and t->prev = prev_t.
   
   So yes, when unfolding dllseg_shape with t as yp, we DO get data_at for t! The issue might be whether the strategies automatically do this.

   Let me also check: does dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) produce data_at for t->next? dlistrep_shape(u, t): unfolds to expose u->next and u->prev = t. Yes, data_at for u->next and u->prev.

   But we also need data_at for t->next. The dllseg_shape would produce it via unfolding. And dlistrep_shape(u, t) would produce u->prev = t. These are different fields (t->next vs u->prev), so no dup_data_at issue.

   Actually wait, there could be a dup_data_at issue. dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next) produces data_at(t->next = u, ...) when unfolding. And dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) = dlistrep_shape(u, t) also produces data_at(u->prev, t). These are different fields (t->next vs u->prev where u = t->next). The issue is: does `t->next` in the first predicate and `u` in the second cause the same data_at to be created twice?

   No. dllseg_shape creates data_at(&(t->next), ...) at address &(t->next). dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) creates data_at(&(t->next->prev), ...) = data_at(&(u->prev), ...) at address &(u->prev). These are different addresses, so no dup.

   But wait, what about the unfolding of dlistrep_shape(u, t) creating data_at(&(u->next), ...)? That's at &(u->next), different again.

Line 30 is `t -> next = y;`. This is an "Assign Exec fail" error. The symexec can't execute this assignment because it can't find the data_at for t->next.

This is a Memory Read error (actually write, but same root cause). t's node doesn't have data_at permission for its next field.

The invariant has `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u)` where t is the yp (last node) parameter. The segment includes t, and when the dllseg_shape is unfolded, it should produce data_at for t->next (= u) and t->prev. But QCP strategies might not automatically unfold the segment to expose t.

Looking at it from the strategy perspective: dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u) is processed by strategies. The strategies need to expose t->next to allow the write `t->next = y`. But t is at the END of the segment, so the strategies would need to unfold the entire segment to reach t, which might not be a standard strategy operation.

The fix: Per SKILL.md fix strategy 1: "Put the dereferenced variable at arg[0] of a shape predicate."

I need t to be arg[0] of a dlistrep_shape. The simplest approach:

`dllseg_shape(x, 0, p, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, p)`

Where p is an exists variable representing t's original prev in the segment. The segment from x to t ends at p (which is t's prev, and p->next = t). dlistrep_shape(t, p) starts at t with prev = p.

But wait, in the pre_loop, t = x. So dllseg_shape(x, 0, p, x) with p = 0 (x->prev = 0). Then emp (x == x and 0 == 0). And dlistrep_shape(x, 0) is the entire x list. But we need u = x->next to also be in the invariant!

Hmm, let me think about this differently.

Actually, the loop body accesses:
1. t->next (write) — needs t as arg[0]
2. y->prev (write) — y is arg[0] of dlistrep_shape(y, 0) ✓
3. u->prev (write) — u needs to be arg[0]
4. y->next (read) — y is arg[0] ✓
5. u->next (read) — u needs to be arg[0]

So both t and u need to be arg[0] of some shape predicate. Currently, u is arg[0] of dlistrep_shape(u, t). But t is NOT arg[0] of anything.

One approach: Split into three parts:

How to fix this? The SKILL.md strategy 4 says: use field expressions on both sides. Let me apply that literally to this case:

The current invariant has:
- dllseg_shape(x, 0, p, t): ends at t
- data_at(t->next, u) and data_at(t->prev, p): boundary node t

After the body, we need:
- dllseg_shape(x, 0, p', u): ends at u
- data_at(u->next, w) and data_at(u->prev, p'): boundary node u

The boundary has shifted from t to u. The dllseg has expanded from ending at t to ending at u.

The recursion unfolds from x. Each step exposes a node x and continues with dllseg_shape(x->next, x, yp, y). The yp stays constant — it's the last node. The recursion stops when the start equals the endpoint.

## Summary

**QCP_VERIFIED: YES** (0 Admitted goals)

**File**: `/home/tzh66/loop-invariant-inference/dataset_qcp_infill/System/GlibC/changed_codes/list/multi_append/multi_append.c`

**Result**: `/home/tzh66/loop-invariant-inference/test_results_20260524_123050/System/GlibC/changed_codes/list/multi_append/result.txt`

### Invariant inferred

The loop traverses the x list with cursors `t` and `u`, inserting y-list nodes one at a time between them:

```c
/*@ Inv Assert exists p,
    x != 0 && t != 0 &&
    dllseg_shape(x, 0, p, t) *
    data_at(&(t->next), struct list*, u) *
    data_at(&(t->prev), struct list*, p) *
    dlistrep_shape(u, t) *
    dlistrep_shape(y, 0) *
    dlistrep_shape(z, 0)
 */
```

### Key design decisions

1. **Explicit `data_at` for boundary node `t`**: `data_at(&(t->next), ..., u)` and `data_at(&(t->prev), ..., p)` give direct permission for `t->next` and `t->prev`, which are the fields written by the loop body (`t->next = y`, etc.). Without these, no dllseg strategy can expose `t`'s fields since dllseg only unfolds from the left (first argument).

2. **`exists p` separates dllseg from data_at**: `p = t->prev` is existentially quantified. The dllseg covers nodes from `x` through `p` (not including `t`), the explicit data_at cover `t`'s fields, and dlistrep covers from `u` onward. This avoids the dup_data_at that occurs when `t` is used as the yp argument of dllseg (which would cause strategy 13 to produce conflicting data_at for `t->next`).

3. **`t != 0` in PROP was the critical fix**: Adding `t != 0` alongside `x != 0` enabled strategies to fire unfold rules during the preservation (`entail_wit_2`) and return (`return_wit`) checks. Without it, 5 Coq witnesses remained admitted despite symexec passing. With it, all witnesses auto-proved (0 Admitted).

### Attempts

- **Attempt 1** (original: `dllseg_shape(x,0,t,u) * dlistrep_shape(u,t)`): Assign Exec fail at `t->next = y` -- no strategy can expose t's fields from within dllseg.
- **Attempt 2** (exists p: `dllseg_shape(x,0,p,t) * dlistrep_shape(t,p)`): "cannot find variable u" -- u not in invariant.
- **Attempt 3** (field expr `dlistrep_shape(t->next,t)`): Assign Exec fail at `u->prev = t` -- field expression re-evaluation issues.
- **Attempt 4** (explicit data_at for t->next only): Symexec passed, but 6 Admitted (dup_data_at on entry).
- **Attempt 5** (explicit data_at for both t->next and t->prev + exists p): Symexec passed, 5 Admitted (preservation and return, no entry dup).
- **Attempt 6** (same + `t != 0`): **0 Admitted -- VERIFIED**.
